        `g4f2` seems to be the strongest move because it's a check and a fork. Even if the king captures (Kxf2), it's a trade-off, but usually, in these positions, the fork is worth it unless there's a mate.

        Is there any attack on Black's king?
        Black King is on f7.
        White Queen is on d3.
        White has a bishop on f1.
        White has a knight on g1.
        White has a bishop on c1.
        White rook on h1.
        White rook on a1.
        Nothing immediately threatening a mate.

        Is there anything better than `g4f2`?
        Let's look at `g4e3`. It attacks the queen.
        If Nf2+, white must move king or capture.
        If Kxf2, Black has lost a knight but White's king is displaced.
        If King moves (e.g., Ke2), Black plays Nxd3 and wins the queen.

        Wait, if Knight moves to f2, and King takes (Kxf2), is there a follow-up?
        Black has a bishop on f8. Black has pawns on e7, g7, h7.
        If Kxf2, maybe... no, not immediately.

        However, winning a queen for a knight is a huge advantage.
        Let's double check the legal moves again: `g4f2` is listed.

    Wait, let me re-read the board one more time.
    Row 2: `♙ ♙ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ♙` (a2, b2, h2) - f2 is definitely empty.

    Row 3: `⭘ ⭘ ♙ ♕ ⭘ ⭘ ♙ ⭘` (c3, d3, g3) - Queen is on d3.
    Knight on g4 moves to f2.
    f2 attacks e1 (K) and d3 (Q) and h1 (R).
    This is a triple fork! It's practically always the best move unless there's a mate.

    One last check on White's pieces.
    Can anyone else take f2?
    - King at e1: Yes, can take f2.
    - Rook at h1: No.
    - Bishop at f1: No.
    - Knight at g1: No.
    - Queen at d3: No. (d3 to f2 is not a queen move).
    Wait, Queen at d3 to f2?
    d3 -> d2 -> f2? No.
    d3 -> e3 -> f2? No.
    d3 to f2 is a knight move, not a queen move.
    So only the King can capture the knight on f2.

    If 1... Nf2+ 2. Kxf2, then Black has lost a knight.
    If 1... Nf2+ 2. Ke2, then 2... Nxd3+ (winning the queen).
    Given the trade-off, Nf2+ is the strongest candidate.
